### historical/auto-gaps.json

Kind `historical`, region `auto-gaps`. Scope: Generated by pipeline/factcheck/coverage_gaps.py — historical polities on Wikidata (with location and dates) that no Cliopatria polygon alive at sample years contains with a matching id or name. Notes only (status known-gap, never applied), ranked by significance; cut-off: score >= 25, life >= 10 years. Regenerate instead of editing; move resolved items into curated files.

Machine-generated file: 420 known-gap entries are not listed here (suggestions for review and gap notes; see the file).

### historical/auto-wikidata.json

Kind `historical`, region `auto-wikidata`. Scope: Generated by pipeline/factcheck/check_cliopatria.py — every Wikidata id used by Cliopatria checked for type (political entity), name, dates and reuse. Active entries change metadata only (set.wikidata); notes need review. Do not edit by hand except to review (set verifiedBy/review/status; reruns keep reviewed entries).

### historical/curated-fixes.json

Kind `historical`, region `curated-fixes`. Scope: Sourced fixes for concrete errors found in the built 1700-1945 data (Cliopatria) by integration review: polities on the wrong islands or continents (Channel Islands, Chukotka, Cayman Islands, Greenland, Cape Colony / Sierra Leone, Hispaniola, Lepanto), unclaimed land that was claimed (Greenland, northern Canada) or indigenous (Hokkaido), and wrong names or kinds (United Kingdom, Kingdom of Ireland, Greece, Congo, Saint-Domingue). Curated by agent:curated-fixes, 2026-10-02.

### historical/northern-america-indigenous.json

Kind `historical`, region `northern-america-indigenous`. Scope: Indigenous nations of North America north of Mexico, 1700-1945 (tier-1 "indigenous" overlays plus fixes to the Cliopatria Haudenosaunee record). Main sources: Royce, Indian Land Cessions in the United States (1899) via the USDA Forest Service digitisation (CC BY 4.0); Royce, The Cherokee Nation of Indians (1887); treaty texts (Kappler); Canadian treaty texts and Library and Archives Canada; Britannica, Handbook of Texas, Canadian Encyclopedia, tribal nation sources and Wikipedia. Audited by agent:audit-h-northern-america-indigenous, 2026-10-01.

Cherokee lands as they stood after the cession to Georgia of 31 May 1783 and before the Treaty of Hopewell (28 Nov 1785): the union of every Royce 1899 area the Cherokee ceded to the United States from 1785 to 1835 (areas 3, 8, 42, 52, 57-59, 64, 76, 79, 83-86, 101-109, 203). Hopewell confirmed the 1783 Georgia line (Tugaloo river - Currahee mountain - head of the south fork of the Oconee), so the union is the 1783-1784 extent. Earlier colonial-era extents are separate entries. Treaty of 22 Jun 1855: the Chickasaw District became the separate Chickasaw Nation, between Island bayou and 98° W, Canadian river and Red river (Royce area 367); the Choctaw and Chickasaw leased the land west of 98° (the Leased District, area 485) to the United States and ceded it in 1866. Allotted to citizens and absorbed into Oklahoma on 16 Nov 1907, so the overlay ends in 1906. The Nation's government continued. Royce dates are treaty signature dates; a cession signed in year Y is shown from Y. | `h-northern-america-indigenous-0053` | assign | `clio:haudenosaunee` — polygon (13 vertices) | 1701–1767 | Cliopatria's Haudenosaunee polygon stops about 78.7° W and leaves the Seneca country between the Genesee, Lake Erie and the Niagara river to New France (around the Niagara portage) or to no polity. Assigned: New York west of 77.9° W outside the Cliopatria polygon, minus the four-mile strip. | `h-northern-america-indigenous-0056` | assign | `clio:haudenosaunee` — polygon (97 vertices) | 1768–1782 | Cliopatria shrinks the Haudenosaunee to the Finger Lakes (about 15,000 km2) from 1769 and gives the rest to the British Colonial Empire, then to the United States. The Treaty of Fort Stanwix (5 Nov 1768) instead fixed a boundary leaving everything west of the line to the Six Nations: New York west of the Owego-Unadilla-Wood creek line (with the Seneca country to the Niagara and Lake Erie and the Mohawk and Oneida hunting grounds north of the Mohawk valley, not ceded) and Pennsylvania north and west of the line (the West Branch Susquehanna, Pine creek and Towanda creek line), which the Six Nations ceded only on 22 Oct 1784 (Royce 1899 area 1). The Revolutionary War did not change title: the Sullivan expedition (1779) burned towns but the land was ceded only after the war. Assigned for 1768-1782: (homeland 1762 + Seneca west) minus the 1768 cession, plus Royce area 1. The Totten and Crossfield purchase of Adirondack land from the Mohawk (1772) is not drawn (no mapped boundary). ### historical/southern-asia.json

Kind `historical`, region `southern-asia`. Scope: Historical audit 1700-1945 of Southern Asia (Natural Earth subregion: Afghanistan, Bangladesh, Bhutan, India, Iran, Maldives, Nepal, Pakistan, Sri Lanka). Compared Cliopatria v0.2.0 coverage maps for sample years 1700-1945 with Wikipedia, Wikidata (every QID verified with the API), the Imperial Gazetteer of India and other cited works; fixes mislabelled and ghost territories, adds missing states, colonial enclaves, princely states and indigenous peoples where sources describe extents, and lists known gaps.

| `h-southern-asia-0739` | assign | `clio:british-raj` — intersection(admin-1 IN-AN + polygon (4 vertices)) | 1859–1941 | Cliopatria's British Raj covers only part of the Andaman group (Little Andaman and outliers stay uncovered and are too far offshore for automatic assignment). The British claimed the whole archipelago from 1858 (Andaman and Nicobar Islands chief commissionership). Assigns the full Andaman group to the Raj; the indigenous overlays stay on top. | [34](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Andaman_and_Nicobar_Islands) [44](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Blair) | medium | active |

| `h-southern-asia-0741` | assign | `clio:british-raj` — intersection(admin-1 IN-AN + polygon (4 vertices)) | 1869–1941 | Nicobar Islands part of British India from 1869 (Danish rights bought 16 October 1868). | `h-southern-asia-0742` | assign | `clio:british-raj` — union(intersection(admin-1 IN-AN + polygon (4 vertices)) + intersection(admin-1 IN-AN + polygon (4 vertices))) | 1945 | Andaman and Nicobar Islands back under British control at the end of 1945 (Japanese surrender signed at Port Blair on 7 October 1945); assigns the whole archipelago to the Raj for the last historical year. | [45](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_occupation_of_the_Andaman_and_Nicobar_Islands) [34](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Andaman_and_Nicobar_Islands) | high | active |

### early/auto-gaps.json

Kind `early`, region `auto-gaps`. Scope: Generated by pipeline/factcheck/coverage_gaps.py — historical polities on Wikidata (with location and dates) that no Cliopatria polygon alive at sample years contains with a matching id or name. Notes only (status known-gap, never applied), ranked by significance; cut-off: score >= 25, life >= 10 years. Regenerate instead of editing; move resolved items into curated files.

Machine-generated file: 487 known-gap entries are not listed here (suggestions for review and gap notes; see the file).

### modern/caribbean-south-america.json

Kind `modern`, region `caribbean-south-america`. Scope: Modern unit timelines 1946–present for the 43 Natural Earth admin-0 units of audit regions caribbean and south-america (ABW AIA ATG BHS BJN BLM BRB CUB CUW CYM DMA DOM GRD HTI JAM KNA LCA MAF MSR PRI SER SXM TCA TTO USG VCT VGB VIR ARG BOL BRA BRI CHL COL ECU FLK GUY PER PRY SPI SUR URY VEN). Researched 2026-10-01: decolonisation (Leeward federal colony, Windward colonies, West Indies Federation 1958–1962, West Indies Associated States 1967–1983, independence dates), the Dutch Caribbean (Territory of Curaçao → Netherlands Antilles 1948 → Kingdom Charter 1954 → Aruba 1986 → dissolution 2010; Surinam → Suriname 1975), French and US territories, Anguilla’s secession 1967–1980, and every Natural Earth disputed area touching these units. De facto control at year end in tier 0; contested areas as tier-1 overlays (0001–0013); editorial decisions, cross-file hand-offs, island assignments the automatic coast rules would get wrong, and known gaps (including Indigenous territories) as note entries (0101–0135). Natural Earth footprints were checked part by part, and the coast rules for uncovered islands were reproduced year by year with the pipeline’s distance function (scripts in .cache/audit/audit-modern-caribbean-south-america/). Wikidata ids verified with wbgetentities/wbsearchentities on 2026-10-01 and all re-checked on 2026-10-02 (second pass: island rules re-measured, the Río de la Plata and Courantyne islands identified, Bolivia/Peru–Chile decision added).

| id | op | polity / target | years | reason | sources | confidence | status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0101` | note |  | 1946–1962 | EDITORIAL — British Caribbean groupings. (1) The Federal Colony of the Leeward Islands was a single colony until 1956, so ATG, KNA, AIA, MSR and VGB show pid ovr:leeward-islands for 1946–1955 (each period names the presidency). Sources differ on the end: the list of governors says the federal colony was dissolved in 1956, its territories becoming colonies in their own right under a single governor (office abolished from 1 January 1960), while the British Leeward Islands and Saint Christopher-Nevis-Anguilla articles say the colony was dissolved or disbanded in 1958, when the federation began. This file follows the 1956 dissolution of the federal colony; the alternative reading would only extend ovr:leeward-islands over 1956–1957. (2) The British Windward Islands were separate colonies under a common governor, so DMA, GRD, LCA and VCT show their own colonial pids with partof “British Windward Islands”. (3) The West Indies Federation (3 Jan 1958 – 31 May 1962) is one polity, ovr:west-indies-federation, for 1958–1961 in ATG, BRB, DMA, GRD, JAM, KNA, AIA, LCA, MSR, TTO, VCT and — attached to Jamaica — CYM and TCA; the British Virgin Islands, Bahamas and British Guiana stayed outside. (4) Associated states (1967–1983) keep the colonial pid (status change, not a new polity) with partof “West Indies Associated States”. (5) Colonies get ovr:colony-of-<name> pids distinct from the later ne: state pids, as packages/borders/AGENTS.md prescribes for dependencies. | [1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Leeward_Islands) [2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_governors_of_the_Leeward_Islands) [3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Christopher-Nevis-Anguilla) [4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Windward_Islands) [5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Indies_Federation) [6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Indies_Associated_States) | high | active |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0102` | note |  | 1946–1957 | EDITORIAL — the Cayman Islands and the Turks and Caicos Islands were dependencies of the Colony of Jamaica until 1959/1962, so CYM and TCA carry pid ovr:colony-of-jamaica for 1946–1957 (same polity, same colour and label as Jamaica) and the West Indies Federation for 1958–1961, becoming separate crown colonies (ne:cym, ne:tca) in 1962. | [7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Cayman_Islands) [8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Turks_and_Caicos_Islands) [5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Indies_Federation) | high | active |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0103` | note |  | 1967–1980 | EDITORIAL — Anguilla 1967–1980 at year end: 1967 and 1968 = Republic of Anguilla (unrecognised; self-governing after expelling the St Kitts police on 30 May 1967; Wikidata Q7314425 dates it 11 July 1967 – 18 March 1969, and the British official of 1968 governed by agreement with the Anguilla Island Council); 1969–1979 = British-administered Anguilla (Commissioner after Operation Sheepskin, 19 March 1969), de jure part of the Associated State; from 19 December 1980 a separate British dependent territory. Overlay m-caribbean-south-america-0012 records the Associated State’s claim. A reviewer could argue 1968 should already be British; that would move one year. | [9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Anguilla) [10](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anguillan_Revolution) [11](https://www.gov.ai/service/about-anguilla/anguilla-history) [3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Christopher-Nevis-Anguilla) | medium | active |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0104` | note |  | 1946–2026 | EDITORIAL — Bajo Nuevo (BJN) and Serranilla (SER) are shown under Colombia in tier 0 for every year: Colombia is the only claimant that has administered them (part of San Andrés y Providencia; lighthouses since 1982, naval garrison on Serranilla’s Beacon Cay) and the ICJ upheld its sovereignty on 19 November 2012. Colombia erected the first lighthouse on Serranilla and the first beacon on Bajo Nuevo in 1982 (the articles give no earlier installation). The US (Guano Islands Act), Nicaragua (1980–2012) and Honduras (Serranilla, named in its constitution of 1982, until it ratified its maritime boundary with Colombia on 20 December 1999) claims are in overlays 0008/0009; Honduras’ earlier claim to Bajo Nuevo and Jamaica’s claim (largely resolved by the 1993 Joint Regime Area) have no verified start dates and are only mentioned in the overlay notes. Natural Earth calls both units Indeterminate. | [12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bajo_Nuevo_Bank) [13](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serranilla_Bank) [14](https://www.icj-cij.org/case/124) | high | active |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0105` | note |  | 1946–2026 | EDITORIAL — Saint-Martin (MAF) and Saint-Barthélemy (BLM) were communes of the overseas department of Guadeloupe from 1946, i.e. part of France, so 1946–2006 shows pid ne:fra “France” (as the rest of Guadeloupe in the FRA unit should). From 15 July 2007 they are overseas collectivities: pids ne:maf / ne:blm, kind dependency, power ne:fra (Natural Earth type Dependency), so they keep France’s colour but get their own labels. If the FRA unit’s editor models Guadeloupe differently for 1946–2006, align these two periods with it. | [15](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guadeloupe) [16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collectivity_of_Saint_Martin) [17](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy) | high | active |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0106` | note |  | 1946–2026 | EDITORIAL — Southern Patagonian Ice Field (SPI): Natural Earth’s unit is the rectangle the 1998 agreement left undefined (49°10′00″–49°47′30″ S). Neither Argentina nor Chile effectively administers it, so tier 0 is a disputed area (pid ovr:southern-patagonian-ice-field, kind disputed) for all years rather than either state, plus overlay 0007 naming both claimants. The wider ice-field boundary was likewise never demarcated before 1998; Natural Earth gives no geometry for that earlier, larger undefined zone, so it is not drawn. | [18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Patagonian_Ice_Field_conflict) [19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentina%E2%80%93Chile_border) | medium | active |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0107` | note |  | 1982 | YEAR-END RULE — the Argentine occupation of the Falklands (2 April – 14 June 1982) and of South Georgia (3–25 April 1982) ended before 31 December 1982, so the map for 1982 shows British control. Recorded so nobody “fixes” 1982. | [20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falkland_Islands_sovereignty_dispute) [21](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invasion_of_South_Georgia) | high | active |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0108` | note |  | 1983–1985 | DECISION — no occupation overlay for Grenada 1983–1985. After the US-led invasion of 25 October 1983 the Governor-General resumed executive authority with an interim advisory council; US combat forces left in December 1983, elections followed in December 1984 and the last US/Caribbean forces left by 1985. Grenada’s administration and sovereignty were not displaced at any year end. | [22](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_invasion_of_Grenada) | medium | active |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0109` | note |  | 1965–1966 | DECISION — no occupation overlay for the Dominican Republic 1965–1966. US forces (from 28 April 1965) and the OAS Inter-American Peace Force (from May 1965) intervened in the civil war, but a Dominican provisional government governed from 3 September 1965 and the last OAS troops left on 21 September 1966, so no area was under foreign administration at 31 December 1965. | [23](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_occupation_of_the_Dominican_Republic_(1965%E2%80%931966%29) | medium | active |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0110` | note |  | 1946–2026 | NOT DRAWN — US bases leased in 1940 under the destroyers-for-bases deal (Antigua, Bahamas, British Guiana near Georgetown, Jamaica, Saint Lucia, Trinidad — e.g. Chaguaramas) were rent-free 99-year leases with sovereignty remaining British; they are small, never a separate territory, and are not drawn (unlike Guantánamo, which Natural Earth maps as its own unit USG). | [24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destroyers-for-bases_deal) [25](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trinidad_and_Tobago) | high | active |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0111` | note |  | 1946–1975 | EDITORIAL — Dutch Caribbean naming: Territory of Curaçao (1946–1947), renamed Netherlands Antilles by the 1948 revision of the Dutch constitution (a new territorial constitution had entered into force in May 1948), constituent country of the Kingdom from 15 December 1954 — one pid ovr:netherlands-antilles with renames as separate periods. Wikidata splits the colony (Q1953333 “Colony of Curaçao and Dependencies”, dissolved 15 December 1954) from the constituent country (Q25227 “Netherlands Antilles”); this file gives Q25227 for every “Netherlands Antilles” period (1948–2009) and no Wikidata id for “Territory of Curaçao” (1946–1947), so that the NLD unit’s Caribbean subunit can repeat exactly the same attributes (note 0122). Surinam: one pid ovr:surinam for the colony (Q7646305, to 14 Dec 1954) and the constituent country (Q19901436, 1954–1975), independent Suriname ne:sur from 25 November 1975. | [26](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colony_of_Cura%C3%A7ao_and_Dependencies) [27](https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao_en_Onderhorigheden) [28](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netherlands_Antilles) [29](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suriname) | high | active |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0112` | note |  | 1946–2026 | VERIFIED (coasts/islands, Caribbean) — every polygon part of the 28 Caribbean units was listed against Natural Earth 10m land (.cache/audit/audit-modern-caribbean-south-america/units-parts.txt), and the coast rules for uncovered islands were reproduced year by year with the pipeline’s own distance function (islands-rule.py in the same folder). Owners match the timelines throughout: ATG = Antigua + Barbuda + Redonda; AIA includes Sombrero; KNA = St Kitts + Nevis; VCT = St Vincent, Bequia, Mustique, Canouan, Union; GRD = Grenada + Carriacou; CUB includes Isla de la Juventud; HTI includes Gonâve and Tortuga; DOM includes Saona and Beata; PRI includes Vieques, Culebra, Mona; the island of Saint Martin is split between MAF and SXM by Natural Earth itself (no majority snapping: the Dutch part is 25%). Uncovered NE parts that the automatic rules give to the right holder every year: Mayreau (4.1 km from VCT, 10.9 km from GRD), Baliceaux and Battowia (VCT), Petite Martinique (2.6 km from GRD, 8.6 km from VCT — won by the 3× ratio), Ronde Island (GRD), Water Island and Hans Lollik (VIR), Cuban cays 0.2–22 km from CUB, Bahamian cays up to 30 km from BHS, Venezuela’s Federal Dependencies islets within 23 km of VEN, the Bajo Nuevo cays (to BJN, i.e. Colombia) and Trinidad’s Bocas islands (note 0121). Left unclaimed by the rules: Bahamian cays more than 30 km from the unit, including Cay Sal Bank (note 0135). Navassa (UMI) and the Swan Islands (HND) belong to other files. | [6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Indies_Associated_States) [30](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_territorial_disputes) [31](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cay_Sal_Bank) | high | active |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0113` | note |  | 1946–2026 | VERIFIED (coasts/islands, South America) — every polygon part of the 15 South American units was listed against Natural Earth 10m land, and uncovered islands were checked against the coast rules (islands-rule.py). Owners match the timelines: Tierra del Fuego split ARG/CHL by Natural Earth (island > 25,000 km², no snapping); CHL includes Easter Island, Juan Fernández, San Félix/San Ambrosio and the Beagle islands; ECU includes the Galápagos and Puná; COL includes San Andrés, Providencia, Roncador, Malpelo and Gorgona; ARG includes Isla de los Estados; GUY includes one Essequibo-estuary island, and Wakenaam and Hogg Island are uncovered parts 2–3 km from GUY with no rival unit (assigned correctly); FLK has 15 parts and its uncovered islets within 30 km (Jason Islands, Sea Lion Island and others) are assigned to it. Uncovered parts the automatic rules leave unclaimed: notes 0125 (Beauchene, Falklands), 0126 (Los Testigos, Genovesa), 0127 (Oyarvide, Solís and Lucía, Argentina), 0129 (Lange Eiland in the Courantyne, Suriname) and 0135 (Diego Ramírez, Chile). Natural Earth has no land for Martín García, Timoteo Domínguez, Trindade, Atol das Rocas, Sala y Gómez, Los Monjes, Isla de Patos, Isla Suárez, Isla Santa Rosa, Ankoko, or the Colombian cays Serrana, Quitasueño, Alburquerque and East-Southeast Cays (claimed by Nicaragua 1980–2012), so none of these can be drawn. | [19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentina%E2%80%93Chile_border) [32](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mart%C3%ADn_Garc%C3%ADa_Island) [33](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Courantyne_River) [14](https://www.icj-cij.org/case/124) | high | active |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0121` | note |  | 1946–2026 | VERIFIED (automatic islands, narrow margin) — Trinidad’s Bocas islands are Natural Earth land outside the TTO admin-0 polygon: Monos/Huevos (4.7 km² at -61.69,10.69; 1.7 km from TTO, 16.8 km from VEN) and Chacachacare (4.1 km² at -61.76,10.68; 8.3 km from TTO, 10.8 km from VEN), distances measured with the pipeline’s km_distance (islands-rule.py). Coast rule (b) gives both to whoever holds TTO each year (colony 1946–1957, West Indies Federation 1958–1961, Trinidad and Tobago from 1962), because TTO is the only polity within uncoveredNearKm (10 km). Chacachacare’s margin is 0.8 km: if uncoveredNearKm is ever raised above 10.8 km it becomes ambiguous (10.8 < 3 × 8.3) and would then need an explicit assignment to TTO. The islands have been Trinidadian throughout (Isla de Patos, the Venezuelan islet in the Dragon’s Mouth, is not NE land). | [25](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trinidad_and_Tobago) | high | active |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0122` | note |  | 1946–2009 | CROSS-FILE — Bonaire, Sint Eustatius and Saba lie in the Natural Earth NLD unit (europe-west file), but 1946–2009 they belonged to the same polity as ABW/CUW/SXM here; from 10 October 2010 they are special municipalities of the Netherlands proper. The NLD unit should carry a subunit with exactly these periods and attributes (the build reports a conflict when one pid has a different name, kind, power or wikidata in the same year): 1946–1947 pid ovr:netherlands-antilles, name “Territory of Curaçao”, kind dependency, power ne:nld, no wikidata; 1948–1953 same pid, name “Netherlands Antilles”, kind dependency, power ne:nld, wikidata Q25227; 1954–2009 as 1948–1953 plus partof “Kingdom of the Netherlands”. | [26](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colony_of_Cura%C3%A7ao_and_Dependencies) [27](https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao_en_Onderhorigheden) [28](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netherlands_Antilles) | high | active |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0123` | note |  | 1946–2026 | CROSS-FILE — Navassa Island (Natural Earth disputed area B34, in the UMI unit of the oceania-antarctica file): administered by the United States, claimed by Haiti (Natural Earth note “Admin. by U.S.A.; Claimed by Haiti”). Haiti is in this file, so the claim is recorded here for the UMI editor; the overlay belongs with the UMI unit. | [34](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haiti) [30](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_territorial_disputes) | high | active |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0124` | note |  | 1946–2026 | CROSS-FILE — claims by states in this file on areas in other units (overlays belong with those units): (a) Argentina claims South Georgia (B32) and the South Sandwich Islands (B33) of the SGS unit (UK-administered). Argentina held South Georgia only 3–25 April 1982 (not visible at year end), but kept the station Corbeta Uruguay on Thule Island (Southern Thule, South Sandwich Islands) from 1976 until it was removed in 1982 — the SGS editor should show Thule Island as Argentine-held for 1976–1981 (subunit, islands point) with a disputed overlay. (b) Suriname claims the Lawa/Marouini headwaters (B14, “Admin. by France; Claimed by Suriname”) in the FRA unit (French Guiana): claimant ne:sur from 1975; whether the Netherlands pressed the claim for Surinam before 1975 (pid ovr:surinam) still needs a source. | [20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falkland_Islands_sovereignty_dispute) [21](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invasion_of_South_Georgia) [35](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Thule) [30](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_territorial_disputes) [29](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suriname) | high | active |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0128` | note |  | 1946–2026 | VERIFIED / NOT DRAWN — Los Monjes (Gulf of Venezuela) are Venezuelan: Colombia recognised Venezuelan sovereignty by note GM-542 of 22 November 1952 and Venezuela occupied them from 29 November 1952 (a Colombian Council of State ruling of 1992 annulled the note domestically). The islets are not Natural Earth land, so nothing is drawn. | [36](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Monjes_Archipelago) | high | active |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0133` | note |  | 1986–2002 | DECISION — internal armed conflicts are not drawn as polities or overlays: Colombia’s El Caguán demilitarised zone (about 42,000 km² in five municipalities of Meta and Caquetá, administered by the FARC from 7 November 1998 until terminated on 20 February 2002, so visible at year ends 1998–2001) and Suriname’s Interior War (22 July 1986 – 8 August 1992, Jungle Commando in eastern Suriname). Sovereignty was never contested internationally and Natural Earth has no municipal (admin-2) geometry for the zone. If the project later shows insurgent-administered areas, the Caguán zone is the clearest candidate (tier-1 overlay, kind other, from official municipal boundaries). | [37](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Cagu%C3%A1n_DMZ) [38](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suriname_Guerrilla_War) | medium | active |
| `m-caribbean-south-america-0134` | note |  | 1946–2026 | NOT DRAWN — two Pacific-coast questions without a drawable area. (1) Bolivia’s aspiration to sovereign access to the Pacific: the Treaty of Peace and Friendship of 20 October 1904 (ratified 10 March 1905) fixed the Bolivia–Chile boundary through 96 points and left the former Litoral (Antofagasta) to Chile; Bolivia has since sought a sovereign outlet to the sea through negotiation rather than claiming a delimited area, and on 1 October 2018 the ICJ found that Chile has no obligation to negotiate one (Bolivia v. Chile, proceedings instituted 24 April 2013). Natural Earth has no disputed area for it and no overlay is drawn on CHL. (2) The Peru–Chile “land triangle” (triángulo terrestre), 37,610 m² (3.7 ha) between Hito 1, where Chile places the start of the land boundary, and Punto Concordia on the shore, where Peru places it: a disagreement dating back to the 1929–1930 demarcation, contested openly since Peru’s baselines law 28621 (November 2005, point 266 = Punto Concordia) and left open by the ICJ judgment of 27 January 2014, which starts the maritime boundary at the parallel of Hito 1 without deciding the land-boundary terminus. Far below Natural Earth resolution, so not drawn. | [39](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Peace_and_Friendship_(1904%29) [40](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obligation_to_Negotiate_Access_to_the_Pacific_Ocean) [41](https://www.icj-cij.org/case/153) [42](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Controversia_entre_Chile_y_Per%C3%BA_sobre_el_tri%C3%A1ngulo_terrestre) [43](https://www.icj-cij.org/case/137) | high | active |

Sources cited in modern/caribbean-south-america.json:

1. [British Leeward Islands (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Leeward_Islands) — Federal Colony of the Leeward Islands 1872–1956; Dominica transferred to the Windward Islands in 1940.
2. [List of governors of the Leeward Islands (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_governors_of_the_Leeward_Islands) — Federal colony dissolved 1956; the members became colonies in their own right under a single governor until 1 January 1960.
3. [Saint Christopher-Nevis-Anguilla (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Christopher-Nevis-Anguilla) — Named Saint Christopher and Nevis 1882–1951 and again from 1980; associated state 1967; independence 19 September 1983.
4. [British Windward Islands (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Windward_Islands) — Not a single colony but separate colonies (Grenada, Saint Lucia, Saint Vincent, Dominica from 1940) under a common governor.
5. [West Indies Federation (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Indies_Federation) — Established 3 January 1958, dissolved 31 May 1962; Cayman Islands and Turks and Caicos attached to Jamaica; British Virgin Islands, Bahamas, British Guiana and British Honduras did not join.
6. [West Indies Associated States (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Indies_Associated_States) — Associated-statehood and independence dates for Antigua, Dominica, Grenada, St Christopher-Nevis-Anguilla, Saint Lucia, Saint Vincent.
7. [History of the Cayman Islands (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Cayman_Islands) — Dependency of Jamaica from 1863; separate Crown dependency after Jamaican independence in 1962.
8. [History of the Turks and Caicos Islands (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Turks_and_Caicos_Islands) — Dependency of Jamaica 1873–1959; crown colony from August 1962; Bahamas governor 1965–1973; own governor 1973.
9. [Republic of Anguilla (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Anguilla) — Referendum and declaration 11 July 1967; interim British official from 8 January 1968; republic proclaimed February 1969; Operation Sheepskin 19 March 1969.
10. [Anguillan Revolution (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anguillan_Revolution)
11. [Government of Anguilla — Anguilla history](https://www.gov.ai/service/about-anguilla/anguilla-history) — Anguilla Day 30 May 1967; separate Dependent Territory from 19 December 1980.
12. [Bajo Nuevo Bank (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bajo_Nuevo_Bank) — Claimed by Colombia (since 1886), the US (Guano Islands Act, 1869), Jamaica and Nicaragua; Honduras had also claimed it before ratifying its maritime boundary with Colombia on 20 December 1999; Colombian beacon since 1982; Colombia–Jamaica fishing agreement 1982–1986 and Joint Regime Area 1993; ICJ 2012 upheld Colombian sovereignty.
13. [Serranilla Bank (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serranilla_Bank) — Colombian naval garrison on Beacon Cay, lighthouse since 1982; US claim 1879–1880, not covered by the 1972 treaty; Honduras claims it in Article 10 of its constitution, but its 1986 maritime boundary with Colombia, ratified 20 December 1999, implicitly recognised Colombian sovereignty; Jamaica’s claim largely resolved by the 1993 Joint Regime Area; ICJ 2012 upheld Colombian sovereignty, accepted by Nicaragua in 2014.
14. [ICJ — Territorial and Maritime Dispute (Nicaragua v. Colombia), case page](https://www.icj-cij.org/case/124) — Preliminary objections judgment 13 December 2007; merits judgment 19 November 2012.
15. [Guadeloupe (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guadeloupe) — Overseas department from 1946; Saint-Martin and Saint-Barthélemy separated by 2007.
16. [Collectivity of Saint Martin (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collectivity_of_Saint_Martin) — Subsumed into the Guadeloupe département in 1946; overseas collectivity from 15 July 2007.
17. [Saint Barthélemy (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy) — Commune of Guadeloupe; overseas collectivity from 15 July 2007; left the EU (OCT) in January 2012.
18. [Southern Patagonian Ice Field dispute (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Patagonian_Ice_Field_conflict) — Agreements of 2 August 1991 and 16 December 1998; undefined rectangle 49°10′00″–49°47′30″ S between Mount Fitz Roy and Cerro Murallón, effectively administered by neither state.
19. [Argentina–Chile border (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentina%E2%80%93Chile_border)
20. [Falkland Islands sovereignty dispute (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falkland_Islands_sovereignty_dispute) — British rule since 1833 except 2 April – 14 June 1982; Argentina’s claim in its 1994 constitution, including South Georgia and the South Sandwich Islands.
21. [Invasion of South Georgia (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invasion_of_South_Georgia) — Argentine forces seized South Georgia on 3 April 1982; retaken 25 April 1982 (Operation Paraquet).
22. [United States invasion of Grenada (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_invasion_of_Grenada) — 25 October 1983; Governor-General Scoon resumed authority; elections December 1984; remaining forces left by 1985.
23. [United States occupation of the Dominican Republic (1965–1966) (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_occupation_of_the_Dominican_Republic_(1965%E2%80%931966%29) — 28 April 1965 – 21 September 1966; provisional government from 3 September 1965.
24. [Destroyers-for-bases deal (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destroyers-for-bases_deal) — 2 September 1940; rent-free 99-year leases; sovereignty remained British.
25. [Trinidad and Tobago (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trinidad_and_Tobago) — Independence 31 August 1962; republic 1 August 1976.
26. [Colony of Curaçao and Dependencies (Wikipedia; “Territory of Curaçao” redirects here)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colony_of_Cura%C3%A7ao_and_Dependencies) — Dutch colony until the Charter for the Kingdom of 15 December 1954; officially the Territory of Curaçao 1936–1948 and the Netherlands Antilles after 1948; a new territorial constitution entered into force in May 1948. Wikidata Q1953333 (dissolved 15 December 1954, followed by Q25227).
27. [Curaçao en Onderhorigheden (Wikipedia, Dutch)](https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao_en_Onderhorigheden) — “Door de herziening van de grondwet in 1948 werd de naam Gebiedsdeel Curaçao vervangen door Nederlandse Antillen” — the 1948 revision of the Dutch constitution replaced the name.
28. [Netherlands Antilles (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netherlands_Antilles) — Charter for the Kingdom 15 December 1954; Aruba seceded 1 January 1986; dissolved 10 October 2010.
29. [Suriname (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suriname) — Constituent country of the Kingdom of the Netherlands from 1954; independence 25 November 1975.
30. [List of territorial disputes (Wikipedia), Americas section](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_territorial_disputes)
31. [Cay Sal Bank (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cay_Sal_Bank) — Westernmost Bahama bank, 23°27′–24°10′N, 79°25′–80°35′W, 50 km from Cuba; administratively part of the Bimini district of The Bahamas.
32. [Martín García Island (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mart%C3%ADn_Garc%C3%ADa_Island) — Argentine (1.84 km²); status fixed by the Río de la Plata Treaty of 19 November 1973; the Uruguayan Timoteo Domínguez emerged as a sandbank in the 1960s and merged with it in the 1980s.
33. [Courantyne River (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Courantyne_River) — Border on the Guyanese (west) bank; the river water body belongs to Suriname (2007 arbitration).
34. [Haiti (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haiti) — Independent 1804; US occupation ended 1934; claims Navassa Island.
35. [Southern Thule (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Thule) — Argentine station Corbeta Uruguay on Thule Island 1976–1982, removed in Operation Keyhole (1982).
36. [Los Monjes Archipelago (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Monjes_Archipelago) — Colombian note GM-542 of 22 November 1952; Venezuelan occupation from 29 November 1952; Colombian Council of State annulled the note in 1992.
37. [El Caguán DMZ (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Cagu%C3%A1n_DMZ) — About 42,000 km² in five municipalities of Meta and Caquetá, FARC-administered from 7 November 1998 until terminated on 20 February 2002.
38. [Surinamese Interior War (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suriname_Guerrilla_War) — 22 July 1986 – 8 August 1992; Jungle Commando (Ronnie Brunswijk) in eastern Suriname; peace treaty 8 August 1992.
39. [Treaty of Peace and Friendship (1904) (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Peace_and_Friendship_(1904%29) — Signed 20 October 1904 (ratifications 10 March 1905); delimits the Bolivia–Chile boundary through 96 points; Antofagasta Chilean.
40. [Obligation to Negotiate Access to the Pacific Ocean (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obligation_to_Negotiate_Access_to_the_Pacific_Ocean) — Bolivia sought sovereign access to the sea; on 1 October 2018 the Court found that Chile had no obligation to negotiate it.
41. [ICJ — Obligation to Negotiate Access to the Pacific Ocean (Bolivia v. Chile), case page](https://www.icj-cij.org/case/153) — Proceedings instituted 24 April 2013; judgment of 1 October 2018 (merits).
42. [Controversia entre Chile y Perú sobre el triángulo terrestre (Wikipedia, Spanish)](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Controversia_entre_Chile_y_Per%C3%BA_sobre_el_tri%C3%A1ngulo_terrestre) — 37,610 m² (3.7 ha) between Hito 1 (Chile’s start of the land boundary) and Punto Concordia (Peru’s), disputed openly since Peru’s baselines law 28621 (November 2005) and again after the ICJ judgment of 27 January 2014. (English Wikipedia’s “37,610 km2” is a unit error.)
43. [ICJ — Maritime Dispute (Peru v. Chile), case page](https://www.icj-cij.org/case/137) — Judgment of 27 January 2014: the maritime boundary starts where the parallel through Boundary Marker No. 1 meets the low-water line; the land boundary terminus was not decided.
44. [Puerto Rico (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerto_Rico) — Unincorporated US territory; Commonwealth constitution 25 July 1952; includes Vieques, Culebra and Mona.
45. [United States Virgin Islands (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Virgin_Islands) — US possession from 31 March 1917; unincorporated organized territory; Revised Organic Act 1954.
46. [Guantanamo Bay Naval Base (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guantanamo_Bay_Naval_Base) — Cuba retains ultimate sovereignty, the US exercises jurisdiction; since 1959 Cuba protests the US presence and has not cashed the rent.
47. [Cuban–American Treaty of Relations (1903) (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuban%E2%80%93American_Treaty_of_Relations_(1903%29)
48. [Cuba (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuba) — Independent 20 May 1902; revolution 1959; since 1959 Cuba regards the Guantánamo base as illegal.
49. [Barbados (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbados) — West Indies Federation 1958–1962; independence 30 November 1966; republic 30 November 2021.
50. [Jamaica (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamaica) — Province of the West Indies Federation from 1958; full independence 6 August 1962.
51. [British Bahamas (Wikipedia; opened via Colony of the Bahamas)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colony_of_the_Bahamas) — Crown colony until independence on 10 July 1973; internal self-government 1964.
52. [List of governors of the Bahamas (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_governors_of_the_Bahamas) — Section 'Governors of the Commonwealth of the Bahama Islands (1969–1973)'.
53. [Bahamas High Commission, London — Political (constitutional history)](https://www.bahamashclondon.net/default/political/) — First written constitution 7 January 1964; new constitution 1969; independence order 1973.
54. [The Bahamas Independence Order 1973 (SI 1973/1080)](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/1973/1080/made) — Comes into operation 10 July 1973; revokes the Bahama Islands (Constitution) Order 1969.
55. [History of the Bahamas (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Bahamas) — Self-government 1964; full independence 10 July 1973.
56. [Grand Cay (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Cay) — Cay south of Walker’s Cay in the Abaco Islands, Bahamas (27.224 N, 78.324 W).
57. [Dominican Republic (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominican_Republic) — Border with Haiti settled 1935–1936; civil war and US intervention 1965.
58. [San Andrés and Providencia Department (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Andr%C3%A9s_and_Providencia_Department) — Colombian since the 19th century; Nicaragua repudiated the 1928 treaty; ICJ 2007 and 2012.
59. [Laguna del Desierto incident (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laguna_del_Desierto_incident) — Clash of 6 November 1965; Argentine occupation afterwards; award to Argentina 21 October 1994, Chile’s appeal refused 1995.
60. [DIFROL (Chile) — Controversia entre los hitos 16 y 17: Arbitraje Palena](https://controversiasinternacionales.difrol.cl/casos/arbitraje-palena/) — Award of Queen Elizabeth II, 9 December 1966: California valley to Chile, 420 km² of mountains to Argentina.
61. [Isla Oyarvide (Wikipedia, Spanish)](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isla_Oyarvide) — Argentine island of the Paraná delta front in the Río de la Plata (partido de San Fernando, Buenos Aires), 34°12′30″S 58°20′30″W, south-west of Martín García across the Canal Buenos Aires; part of the delta’s advancing front of sediment banks and islands.
62. [Islas Lucía (Wikipedia, Spanish)](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islas_Luc%C3%ADa) — Argentine (partido de San Fernando), 34°10′S 58°20′W; under Argentine sovereignty after the 1973 Río de la Plata Treaty; named by the Argentine Naval Hydrographic Service on 15 December 2001; recent alluvial formation.
63. [Islas Solís (Wikipedia, Spanish)](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islas_Sol%C3%ADs) — About 6 km south-west of Martín García, next to Oyarvide and Lucía, all Argentine (San Fernando); recent sedimentary formation.
64. [Bolivia (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolivia) — Chaco losses settled 1938; official name Plurinational State of Bolivia under the 2009 constitution.
65. [Isla Suárez / Ilha de Guajará-Mirim (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isla_Su%C3%A1rez) — Bolivian administration in the Mamoré; Brazilian complaints since at least 1930.
66. [Rincón de Artigas (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rinc%C3%B3n_de_Artigas) — Effectively under Brazilian control.
67. [Rincón de Artigas (Wikipedia en español)](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rinc%C3%B3n_de_Artigas) — 237 km²; Uruguay has asked Brazil to review the Arroyo de la Invernada since 1934.
68. [Guaíra Falls (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gua%C3%ADra_Falls) — Both Brazil and Paraguay claimed the falls; Itaipu treaty 1973; falls flooded 1982.
69. [Blanc, J., 'Itaipu's Forgotten History: The 1965 Brazil–Paraguay Border Crisis', Journal of Latin American Studies 50(2), 2018 (abstract)](https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/journal-of-latin-american-studies/article/abs/itaipus-forgotten-history-the-1965-brazilparaguay-border-crisis-and-the-new-geopolitics-of-the-southern-cone/7CDDBAD86D8F8B665E53C3C4BEB74F4D) — Fifteen-month border conflict, March 1965 – June 1966 (Act of Iguaçu).
70. [Indigenous territory (Brazil) (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_territory_(Brazil%29) — About 724 territories (2020) covering about 13% of Brazil; Yanomami homologated 1992, Xingu park 1961, Raposa Serra do Sol 2005.
71. [Brazilian Island (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Island) — Administered by Brazil (Barra do Quaraí); claimed by Uruguay (Bella Unión, Artigas).
72. [Isla Brasilera (Wikipedia en español)](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isla_Brasilera) — Uruguay claims it since 1940 (formal protest of 8 September 1940).
73. [Beagle conflict (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beagle_conflict) — Picton, Lennox and Nueva under Chilean control; Argentine claim from 1904; award 2 May 1977, rejected 25 January 1978; treaty of 29 November 1984.
74. [Diego Ramírez Islands (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diego_Ram%C3%ADrez_Islands) — Small group of Chilean subantarctic islands at about 56.48 S, 68.73 W.
75. [Esguerra–Bárcenas Treaty (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esguerra-B%C3%A1rcenas_Treaty) — Signed 24 March 1928, in force 1930; Nicaragua’s junta declared it invalid in 1980.
76. [Roncador Bank (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roncador_Bank) — US Guano Islands Act claim; ceded to Colombia 17 September 1981 under the Vásquez-Saccio Treaty (signed 1972).
77. [Santa Rosa Island (Amazon River) (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Rosa_Island_(Amazon_River%29) — Peruvian administration (RENIEC office since 2004; district created 3 July 2025 by Law 32403); claimed by Colombia (Leticia).
78. [Ecuadorian–Peruvian territorial dispute (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecuadorian%E2%80%93Peruvian_territorial_dispute) — Rio Protocol 29 January 1942; nullity declared by Velasco Ibarra in 1960; Paquisha 1981; Cenepa 1995; Brasília Presidential Act 26 October 1998.
79. [US Department of State, International Boundary Study No. 172: Ecuador – Peru (1980)](https://library.law.fsu.edu/Digital-Collections/LimitsinSeas/pdf/ibs172.pdf)
80. [Guyana (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guyana) — Independence 26 May 1966; republic 23 February 1970; Venezuela revived its Essequibo claim in 1962; Tigri incidents 1967–1969.
81. [Guayana Esequiba (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guayana_Esequiba) — Venezuela denounced the 1899 award at the UN on 12 November 1962; Geneva Agreement 17 February 1966; ICJ referral 2018; Venezuelan referendum 2023 and law 2024.
82. [Tigri Area (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tigri_Area) — Administered by Guyana; 1936 commission gave the river to Suriname and the triangle to British Guiana (treaty never ratified); Camp Tigri taken by Guyana 19 August 1969.
83. [The Guyana–Suriname Boundary: A Historical Review (guyananews.org)](http://www.guyananews.org/suriname/guysuri_boundary.html) — 1799 agreement placed the Courantyne islands under Suriname; Suriname officially claimed the New River Triangle for the first time in 1962.
84. [Paraguay (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paraguay)
85. [Uruguay (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uruguay)
86. [Venezuela (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuela)
87. [Aves Island (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aves_Island) — 1865 arbitration for Venezuela; Venezuelan naval base from 2 June 1978; Dominica’s PM recognised Venezuelan sovereignty in June 2006.
88. [Constitución de la República de Honduras (1982) — Political Database of the Americas, Georgetown University](https://pdba.georgetown.edu/Constitutions/Honduras/hond05.html) — Decree 131 of 11 January 1982; Article 10 lists, among Honduran territories, “los Bancos Salmedina, providencia, De Coral, Cabo Falso, Rosalinda y Serranilla”.
89. [Treaty of Peace and Friendship of 1984 between Chile and Argentina (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Peace_and_Friendship_of_1984_between_Chile_and_Argentina) — Signed 29 November 1984; ratifications exchanged (entry into force) 2 May 1985; Argentina accepted the 1977 award line.

### modern/europe-east-central-asia.json

Kind `modern`, region `europe-east-central-asia`. Scope: Modern unit timelines 1946–present for the 16 Natural Earth admin-0 units of audit regions eastern-europe, russia and central-asia (BGR BLR CZE HUN MDA POL ROU SVK UKR RUS KAZ KAB KGZ TJK TKM UZB). Researched 2026-10-01/02: post-war regime changes and renames (Bulgaria 1946/1990, Romania 1947/1965/1989, Hungary 1946/1949/1989, Poland 1952/1989, Czechoslovakia 1960/1990 and its dissolution 1992/93), the Soviet Union (1946–1990 in tier 0; dissolved 26 December 1991, so its successors appear from 1991), territory that changed hands inside these units (Jäniskoski 1947, the Polish–Soviet exchange of 1951, Crimea 1954/1991/2014, Baikonur lease 1994), breakaway and occupied areas (Chechnya/Ichkeria 1991–1999, Transnistria 1991–, the Donetsk and Luhansk People’s Republics 2014–2021, Russian-held Ukraine 2022– from DeepStateMap year-end snapshots, the Ukrainian-held part of Kursk Oblast 2024, the southern Kurils). De facto control at year end in tier 0; contested areas as tier-1 overlays (0001–0013); editorial decisions, cross-file hand-offs, island problems and known gaps as note entries (0101–0117). Scripts and data in .cache/audit/audit-modern-europe-east-central-asia/. Wikidata ids verified with wbgetentities/wbsearchentities on 2026-10-01/02.

| id | op | polity / target | years | reason | sources | confidence | status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `m-europe-east-central-asia-0101` | note |  | 1946–1991 | EDITORIAL — end year of the Soviet Union. The shared-pid table lists ovr:soviet-union as “1946–1991”, but the Soviet Union was formally dissolved on 26 December 1991 (Declaration 142-N) and every republic in this file had declared independence by 16 December 1991. Under the year-end rule (the map for year Y shows 31 December of Y) the 1991 map therefore shows the successor states, so the Soviet periods here run 1946–1990 and Russia, Ukraine, Belarus, Moldova, Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Tajikistan, Turkmenistan and Uzbekistan start in 1991. Other files with Soviet units (Baltic states, Caucasus) should end the Soviet period in 1990 as well; the pid, name “Soviet Union” and Wikidata Q15180 follow the table exactly. Transfers between union republics (e.g. Crimea 1954) do not change the polity and are not drawn before 1991. | [1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union) [2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-Soviet_states) | high | active |
| `m-europe-east-central-asia-0102` | note |  | 1946–2026 | EDITORIAL — regime names and renames. Present-day states keep one ne: pid across their post-war regimes, each official name being a separate period: Bulgaria (People’s Republic 1946–1989, Bulgaria 1990–), Romania (Kingdom 1946, Romanian People’s Republic 1947–1964, Socialist Republic 1965–1988, Romania 1989–), Hungary (Republic 1946–1948, People’s Republic 1949–1988, Republic 1989–), Poland (Republic of Poland 1946–1951, Polish People’s Republic 1952–1988, Poland 1989–), Czech Republic 1993–2015 → Czechia 2016–. Czechoslovakia uses the shared pid ovr:czechoslovakia with the name “Czechoslovakia” throughout and its official names as altNames per period. The Polish 1946–1951 period and the Polish subunit in UKR use one identical state object so the pipeline dissolves them into one record. | [3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People%27s_Republic_of_Bulgaria) [4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Republic_of_Romania) [5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian_People%27s_Republic) [6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_People%27s_Republic) [7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_Czechoslovakia) [8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Name_of_the_Czech_Republic) | high | active |
| `m-europe-east-central-asia-0103` | note |  | 2022–2026 | METHOD — Russian-held Ukraine 2022–present. Tier 0 shows de facto Russian control at year end. Geometry: DeepStateMap year-end snapshots (31 Dec 2022, 2023, 2024, 2025; 30 Sep 2026 for “present”), union of the layers “Окуповано” (occupied since 2022), “ОРДЛО” (held before 24 February 2022) and occupied Crimea; intersected with Natural Earth’s UKR unit; strips narrower than about 1 km between that area and the Russian border, the Crimean boundary or the coast removed (outline mismatches); simplified to about 1 km and extended a few km across the international border/coast so that the unit clip draws those edges. Result inside UKR: 80,330 km² (2022), 80,558 (2023), 83,825 (2024), 88,043 (2025), 88,852 (Sept 2026); difference from the raw layers 0.6–0.8 %. Grey “status unknown” zones along the front (500–1,400 km²) and areas marked liberated are left with Ukraine. Includes the Kinburn spit and the Russian-held edges of Kharkiv, Sumy and Dnipropetrovsk oblasts. Earlier situations within a year (the March 2022 advance on Kyiv, Kherson city until 11 November 2022, Snake Island until June 2022) are not shown, per the year-end rule. | [9](https://deepstatemap.live/) [10](https://deepstatemap.live/api/history/1672528847/geojson) [11](https://deepstatemap.live/api/history/1704057558/geojson) [12](https://deepstatemap.live/api/history/1735678434/geojson) [13](https://deepstatemap.live/api/history/1767200597/geojson) [14](https://deepstatemap.live/api/history/1790798080/geojson) [15](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts) | medium | active |
| `m-europe-east-central-asia-0104` | note |  | 2014–2021 | EDITORIAL — Donetsk and Luhansk People’s Republics 2014–2021 are tier-0 “state” periods (de facto separate administrations, unrecognised) rather than Russian dependencies, because Russia did not claim or annex them before 2022; the polity notes record that they are widely described as Russian puppet states. Geometry is Natural Earth’s C02/C03 (about a third of each oblast). From 2022 (recognition 21 February, annexation 30 September) the whole Russian-held area is shown as Russia. | [16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donetsk_People%27s_Republic) [17](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhansk_People%27s_Republic) [15](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts) [18](https://www.naturalearthdata.com/downloads/10m-cultural-vectors/) | medium | active |
| `m-europe-east-central-asia-0106` | note |  | 1991–2000 | EDITORIAL — Chechnya at year end. 1991–1994 Ichkeria (independence declared 1 November 1991; Russian troops entered on 11 December 1994, Grozny still held at year end); 1995 Russia (Grozny taken January–March, most lowlands by May); 1996–1999 Ichkeria (Khasavyurt Accord, federal withdrawal by 31 December 1996); 1999 is a judgement: Russian forces re-entered on 1 October 1999 and held the northern lowlands and Gudermes at year end, but Grozny resisted until 6 February 2000, so the de facto state is still shown for 1999 and Russia from 2000. Natural Earth’s RU-CE (today’s Chechen Republic) stands for Ichkeria’s territory after the Chechen–Ingush separation of 1992; the disputed Sunzha district is not modelled. Overlay 0013 marks 1991–1999 as contested. | [19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chechen_Republic_of_Ichkeria) [20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Chechen_War) [21](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Chechen_War) | medium | active |
| `m-europe-east-central-asia-0108` | note |  | 1990–1994 | NOT DRAWN — Gagauzia. Comrat declared a Gagauz Soviet republic on 19 August 1990, annulled by Moldova; the dispute ended with the Law on the Special Legal Status of Gagauzia (23 December 1994, in force 14 January 1995). Its separate status was never an armed separation comparable to Transnistria and no source gives an extent of its de facto control, so it stays part of Moldova (and of the Soviet Union in 1990). | [22](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gagauzia) | medium | active |
| `m-europe-east-central-asia-0109` | note |  | 1946–1990 | NOT DRAWN — Oder–Neisse territories and Kaliningrad. The Potsdam Agreement placed the German lands east of the Oder–Neisse line under Polish administration and northern East Prussia (Königsberg/Kaliningrad) under the Soviet Union pending a peace settlement; East Germany recognised the line in 1950, West Germany accepted it with reservations in 1970, and the German–Polish Border Treaty of 14 November 1990 made it final. De facto control was Polish/Soviet from 1945, so these areas are Poland and the Soviet Union in tier 0 from 1946 without a disputed overlay (West Germany’s legal reservation was not a territorial occupation or an active claim in the sense of policy §4.4). | [23](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oder%E2%80%93Neisse_line) | high | active |
| `m-europe-east-central-asia-0110` | note |  | 1991–2007 | NOT DRAWN — Estonian and Latvian claims on Russian-held areas. Pechory/Izborsk and the land east of the Narva (annexed to the Russian SFSR in 1944) and Abrene/Pytalovo (transferred 16 January 1945) were Soviet and then Russian throughout 1946–present. After 1991 Estonia argued for the 1920 Tartu line (border treaties of 2005 and 2014 signed but not ratified) and Latvia claimed Abrene until the treaty of 27 March 2007. These were diplomatic positions without contested control, so no overlay is drawn; a reviewer may add tier-1 overlays from Cliopatria’s 1939 Estonia/Latvia records if desired. | [24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estonia%E2%80%93Russia_border) [25](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latvia%E2%80%93Russia_border) | medium | active |
| `m-europe-east-central-asia-0112` | note |  | 1946–2026 | NOT DRAWN — Snake Island (Zmiinyi, 0.17 km², 45.255°N 30.204°E): Romanian until the Romanian–Soviet protocol of 4 February 1948, Soviet, then Ukrainian from 1991, held by Russia from 24 February to late June 2022. It is not part of Natural Earth’s 10m land or of the UKR unit, so no subunit is possible; the year-end owner would have been Romania for 1946–1947 only. | [26](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snake_Island_(Black_Sea%29) | high | active |
| `m-europe-east-central-asia-0114` | note |  | 1946–2008 | CROSS-FILE — Amur/Ussuri islands. Natural Earth already draws the 2004 line across Bolshoy Ussuriysky Island (checked on a plot of the admin-0 boundaries near Khabarovsk), so the western part of that island and Tarabarov (Yinlong) Island, about 174 km² held by the Soviet Union from 1929 and by Russia until the handover of 14 October 2008, lie in the CHN unit. That unit needs Soviet Union (1946–1990) and Russia (1991–2007) periods for them; nothing changes inside the RUS unit. | [27](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolshoy_Ussuriysky_Island) | high | active |
| `m-europe-east-central-asia-0116` | note |  | 1946–1947 | VERIFIED — 1946 footprint of the RUS unit. Areas of today’s Russia that changed hands around 1944–1947 were already Soviet by 1946 (Kaliningrad, Pechory/Izborsk, the land east of the Narva, Pytalovo, the southern Kurils) except the Jäniskoski–Niskakoski area, Finnish until its sale on 18 April 1947, which is the subunit janiskoski-1947 (approximate; 176.2 km² against the documented 176 km²). Porkkala (leased 1944–1956) lies in Natural Earth’s FIN unit (another file). | [28](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%A4niskoski-Niskakoski_territory) [23](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oder%E2%80%93Neisse_line) [24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estonia%E2%80%93Russia_border) [25](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latvia%E2%80%93Russia_border) [29](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuril_Islands_dispute) | high | active |
| `m-europe-east-central-asia-0117` | note |  | 2024–2026 | EDITORIAL — Ukrainian-held land in Russia. Only the end-2024 Kursk salient (DeepStateMap blue layer, 488 km² after simplification; a further 157 km² grey zone not counted) is drawn, as a RUS subunit held by Ukraine with overlay 0009. Russia retook Sudzha on 12 March 2025 and declared the offensive over on 26 April 2025. Later Ukrainian positions on Russian soil shown by DeepState (about 11 km² near Tyotkino, Kursk Oblast, at the end of 2025 and in September 2026) are below the dataset’s granularity and are not drawn. | [30](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Kursk_offensive) [9](https://deepstatemap.live/) [12](https://deepstatemap.live/api/history/1735678434/geojson) [13](https://deepstatemap.live/api/history/1767200597/geojson) | medium | active |
