Sources cited in modern/europe-east-central-asia.json:

1. [Dissolution of the Soviet Union (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union) — The Soviet of the Republics formally dissolved the Soviet Union by Declaration No. 142-N on 26 December 1991; Gorbachev resigned on 25 December; Belovezha Accords 8 December, Alma-Ata Protocol 21 December 1991.
2. [Post-Soviet states (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-Soviet_states) — Independence: Ukraine 24 Aug 1991, Belarus 25 Aug, Moldova 27 Aug, Kyrgyzstan 31 Aug, Uzbekistan 1 Sep, Tajikistan 9 Sep, Turkmenistan 27 Oct, Russia 12 Dec, Kazakhstan 16 Dec 1991; Transnistria (declared 2 Sep 1990) is unrecognised and internationally part of Moldova.
3. [People's Republic of Bulgaria (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People%27s_Republic_of_Bulgaria) — Monarchy abolished by the plebiscite of September 1946 and the republic proclaimed on 15 September 1946; Dimitrov Constitution December 1947; renamed Republic of Bulgaria on 15 November 1990; the People’s Republic had the same borders as present-day Bulgaria.
4. [Socialist Republic of Romania (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Republic_of_Romania) — King Michael forced to abdicate and the monarchy abolished on 30 December 1947; renamed Socialist Republic of Romania on 21 August 1965; name changed to Romania on 28 December 1989.
5. [Hungarian People's Republic (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian_People%27s_Republic) — Second Hungarian Republic from 1 February 1946; renamed Hungarian People’s Republic by the constitution promulgated on 20 August 1949; Republic of Hungary proclaimed on 23 October 1989.
6. [Polish People's Republic (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_People%27s_Republic) — Polish People’s Republic 1947–1989; the official name was Republic of Poland (Rzeczpospolita Polska) under the Small Constitution of 1947 until the Constitution of 1952 introduced “People’s Republic”; constitution amended on 29 December 1989.
7. [Dissolution of Czechoslovakia (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_Czechoslovakia) — Czechoslovakia ceased to exist on 31 December 1992; official names: Czechoslovak Republic, Czechoslovak Socialist Republic (from 11 July 1960), Czechoslovak Federative Republic (March 1990), Czech and Slovak Federative Republic (April 1990).
8. [Name of the Czech Republic (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Name_of_the_Czech_Republic) — Short name “Czechia” endorsed 14 April 2016, approved by the cabinet 2 May 2016, entered in UN databases 5 July 2016; the full name remains Czech Republic.
9. [DeepStateMap.Live — interactive map of the war in Ukraine (public history API, year-end snapshots)](https://deepstatemap.live/) — Layers used: “Окуповано” (occupied since 2022), “ОРДЛО” (held before 24 February 2022), “Окупований Крим”; “Статус невідомий” (status unknown) and “Звільнено” (liberated) were not counted as Russian-held. Snapshots: 2022-12-31 id 1672528847, 2023-12-31 id 1704057558, 2024-12-31 id 1735678434, 2025-12-31 id 1767200597, 2026-09-30 id 1790798080.
10. [DeepStateMap snapshot 2022-12-31 (GeoJSON, history id 1672528847)](https://deepstatemap.live/api/history/1672528847/geojson) — Year-end 2022 control polygons as published by DeepState.
11. [DeepStateMap snapshot 2023-12-31 (GeoJSON, history id 1704057558)](https://deepstatemap.live/api/history/1704057558/geojson) — Year-end 2023 control polygons as published by DeepState.
12. [DeepStateMap snapshot 2024-12-31 (GeoJSON, history id 1735678434)](https://deepstatemap.live/api/history/1735678434/geojson) — Year-end 2024 control polygons as published by DeepState.
13. [DeepStateMap snapshot 2025-12-31 (GeoJSON, history id 1767200597)](https://deepstatemap.live/api/history/1767200597/geojson) — Year-end 2025 control polygons as published by DeepState.
14. [DeepStateMap snapshot 2026-09-30 (GeoJSON, history id 1790798080)](https://deepstatemap.live/api/history/1790798080/geojson) — Year-end 2026 control polygons as published by DeepState.
15. [Russian annexation of Donetsk, Kherson, Luhansk and Zaporizhzhia oblasts (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts) — Referendums 23–27 September 2022, accession treaties 30 September 2022; UN General Assembly resolution ES-11/4 (12 October 2022, 143–5–35) declared them invalid; Ukraine retook Kherson city on 11 November 2022.
16. [Donetsk People's Republic (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donetsk_People%27s_Republic) — Declared 7 April 2014 (referendum 11 May 2014); about a third of Donetsk Oblast held 2015–2022; recognised by Russia 21 February 2022; annexed by Russia 30 September 2022; described as a puppet state of Russia.
17. [Luhansk People's Republic (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhansk_People%27s_Republic) — Proclaimed 27 April 2014, independence declared 12 May 2014; about a third of Luhansk Oblast held 2015–2022; Russia recognised it 21 February 2022, captured the rest of the oblast by July 2022 (Lysychansk) and annexed it 30 September 2022.
18. [Natural Earth 1:10m cultural vectors — admin-0 countries, admin-1 states/provinces, breakaway and disputed areas](https://www.naturalearthdata.com/downloads/10m-cultural-vectors/) — Unit footprints and the disputed areas B29 (Kuril Is.), B36 (Transnistria), B40 (Baykonur), B89 (Crimea), C02 (Donetsk People’s Republic), C03 (Luhansk People’s Republic).
19. [Chechen Republic of Ichkeria (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chechen_Republic_of_Ichkeria) — Unilateral declaration of independence 1 November 1991; Chechnya and Ingushetia separated 4 June 1992; First Chechen War from 11 December 1994; Russia–Chechen Peace Treaty 12 May 1997; de facto independent 1996–1999; Russian campaign from 1 October 1999; Grozny fell 6 February 2000; Russian-appointed administration from June 2000.
20. [First Chechen War (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Chechen_War) — Russian troops entered on 11 December 1994; presidential palace taken 19 January 1995, fighting for Grozny until 6 March 1995; most lowland areas conquered January–May 1995; Chechen recapture of Grozny from 6 August 1996; Khasavyurt Accord 31 August 1996 required the withdrawal of all federal forces by 31 December 1996.
21. [Second Chechen War (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Chechen_War) — Siege of Grozny December 1999 – February 2000; Russian direct rule over Chechnya from May 2000.
22. [Gagauzia (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gagauzia) — Comrat declared a separate Gagauz Soviet republic on 19 August 1990 (annulled by Moldova); the Law on the Special Legal Status of Gagauzia (23 December 1994, in force 14 January 1995) reintegrated it as an autonomous unit.
23. [Oder–Neisse line (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oder%E2%80%93Neisse_line) — Potsdam (2 August 1945): territories east of the line under Polish administration pending a peace settlement; recognised by East Germany (Zgorzelec, 6 July 1950), accepted by West Germany with reservations (Warsaw, 7 December 1970), final German–Polish Border Treaty 14 November 1990; northern East Prussia with Königsberg annexed by the USSR as Kaliningrad Oblast.
24. [Estonia–Russia border (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estonia%E2%80%93Russia_border) — Pechory, Izborsk and the area east of the Narva annexed to the Russian SFSR in 1944; after 1991 Estonia argued for the 1920 Tartu line; 2005 treaty not ratified by Russia; 2014 treaty signed without the disputed preamble, not ratified.
25. [Latvia–Russia border (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latvia%E2%80%93Russia_border) — Abrene county transferred from the Latvian SSR to Pskov Oblast on 16 January 1945 (Pytalovsky District); the border treaty of 27 March 2007 recognised it as part of Russia.
26. [Snake Island (Black Sea) (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snake_Island_(Black_Sea%29) — 0.17 km²; placed under Soviet administration by the Romanian–Soviet protocol of 4 February 1948; Ukrainian after 1991; held by Russia from 24 February to late June 2022.
27. [Bolshoy Ussuriysky Island (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolshoy_Ussuriysky_Island) — Occupied by the Soviet Union in 1929; Complementary Agreement 14 October 2004; about 174 km² (the western part of the island and Yinlong/Tarabarov Island) transferred to China on 14 October 2008.
28. [Jäniskoski-Niskakoski territory (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%A4niskoski-Niskakoski_territory) — A 176 km² area east of Lake Inari along the Paatsjoki, sold by Finland to the Soviet Union on 18 April 1947 (treaty of 3 February 1947) for the Jäniskoski hydro-electric plant; separate from the Petsamo cession.
29. [Kuril Islands dispute (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuril_Islands_dispute) — Iturup, Kunashir, Shikotan and the Habomai Islands (Japan’s Northern Territories) were taken by Soviet forces between 18 August and 3 September 1945 and incorporated in early 1946; Japan maintains its claim; the 1956 offer of Shikotan and the Habomais was not concluded.
30. [2024 Kursk offensive (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Kursk_offensive) — Ukrainian incursion from 6 August 2024 (about 1,250 km² by 19 August); by the end of December 2024 Russia had retaken roughly half; Russian troops entered Sudzha on 12 March 2025; Russia declared the offensive defeated on 26 April 2025.
31. [Rusovce (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rusovce) — On 15 October 1947, together with Čunovo and Jarovce, Rusovce was annexed by Czechoslovakia according to the Paris Peace Treaty; area 25.55 km²; borough of Bratislava since 1 January 1972.
32. [Transnistria (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transnistria) — Declared a Soviet republic on 2 September 1990, independence 25 August 1991, PMR formally established 5 November 1991; war 2 March – 1 July 1992, ceasefire 21 July 1992; six left-bank communes stay under Moldova while Bender and four right-bank communes are held by the PMR; about 4,163 km²; unrecognised but de facto independent.
33. [Transnistria War (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transnistria_War) — PMR forces held Dubăsari against Moldovan police (November 1990, 13 December 1991); in late 1991 the police in Tiraspol and Rîbnița swore allegiance to the PMR; in June 1992 Moldovan forces entered Bender and PMR forces retook most of the city including the centre.
34. [1951 Polish–Soviet territorial exchange (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1951_Polish%E2%80%93Soviet_territorial_exchange) — Signed 15 February 1951, ratified by Poland 28 May and by the USSR 31 May, in force 5 June 1951; about 480 km² each way: Poland gave the Sokal land (Bełz, Krystynopol, Uhnów, the Rawa Ruska–Krystynopol railway) and received part of Drohobych Oblast (Ustrzyki Dolne, Czarna, Lutowiska, Krościenko, Bandrów Narodowy, Bystre, Liskowate).
35. [Umowa o zmianie granic z 15 lutego 1951 (Polish Wikipedia)](https://pl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umowa_o_zmianie_granic_z_15_lutego_1951) — Poland ceded Bełz, Uhnów, Krystynopol, Waręż, Chorobrów and the left-bank part of Sokal (Żwirka); the USSR ceded the lands north-east of the upper San with Ustrzyki Dolne, Czarna, Lutowiska and Krościenko; 480 km².
36. [Umowa graniczna pomiędzy Polską a ZSRR z 16 sierpnia 1945 roku — treaty text (Polish Wikisource)](https://pl.wikisource.org/wiki/Umowa_graniczna_pomi%C4%99dzy_Polsk%C4%85_a_ZSRR_z_16_sierpnia_1945_roku) — Article 2: from the source of the San down the San (mid-stream) to a point south of Solina, then east of Przemyśl and west of Rawa Ruska to the Sołokija river — the line in force until 1951.
37. [Umowa o zmianie granic między Polską a ZSRR (1951) — treaty text (Polish Wikisource)](https://pl.wikisource.org/wiki/Umowa_o_zmianie_granic_mi%C4%99dzy_Polsk%C4%85_a_ZSRR_(1951%29) — Describes the new (today’s) line: in the Lublin section it leaves Uhnów, Bełz, Waręż etc. to the USSR and ends on the Bug; in the Drohobych section it leaves Lutowiska, Bystre, Bandrów-Kolonia, Krościenko and Liskowate to Poland and ends near Jureczkowa/Łopusznica.
38. [Annexation of Crimea by the Russian Federation (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annexation_of_Crimea_by_the_Russian_Federation) — Crimean Oblast transferred from the Russian SFSR to the Ukrainian SSR in 1954; Russian special forces seized Crimea from 27 February 2014; Russia incorporated the Republic of Crimea and Sevastopol on 18 March 2014; UN General Assembly resolution 68/262 affirmed Ukraine’s territorial integrity.
39. [China–Kazakhstan border (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93Kazakhstan_border) — Treaty of 26 April 1994, further agreements 24 September 1997 and 4 July 1998; a strip east of Lake Zhalanashkol contested in 1969 became Chinese; demarcation protocol 10 May 2002.
40. [Baikonur Cosmodrome (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baikonur_Cosmodrome) — Founded 1955 at Tyuratam; leased by Russia after 1991 for US$115 million a year for 20 years with a 10-year option; on 8 June 2005 the Russian Federation Council ratified the extension of the lease to 2050; a spaceport operated by Russia within Kazakhstan.
41. [Baikonur (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baikonur) — Leased and administered by the Russian Federation until 2050; the Russian-controlled area is an ellipse of 90 km (east–west) by 85 km (north–south); the town (Leninsk) was renamed Baikonur on 20 December 1995.
42. [Kazakhstan’s President to demand Baikonur rent from Russia (Jamestown Foundation, Monitor)](https://jamestown.org/kazakhstans-president-to-demand-baikonur-rent-from-russia/) — Russia leased the cosmodrome for twenty years with an optional ten-year extension at US$115 million a year; lease signed with Kazakhstan in December 1994.
43. [China–Kyrgyzstan border (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93Kyrgyzstan_border) — Delimited in 1996; finalised in 2009 with Kyrgyzstan ceding the Uzengi-Kush area south of Issyk-Kul and China part of the Khan Tengri massif.
44. [China–Tajikistan border (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93Tajikistan_border) — About 28,500 km² disputed after 1991; Tajikistan ratified in 2011 the 1999 agreement and the 2002 supplementary agreement ceding about 200 and 1,122 km² in the Pamirs; China relinquished claims to over 28,000 km².
45. [Tajikistan cedes disputed land to China (Jamestown Foundation, Eurasia Daily Monitor, January 2011)](https://jamestown.org/tajikistan-cedes-disputed-land-to-china/) — China had claimed the area south of the Uz-Bel pass including Lake Rangkul in eastern Murghab district; the lower house ratified the 2002 agreement on 12 January 2011 (1,122 km²).

### modern/north-central-america.json

Kind `modern`, region `north-central-america`. Scope: Modern unit timelines 1946–present for the Natural Earth admin-0 units of the audit regions northern-america and central-america: BLZ, CLP, CRI, GTM, HND, MEX, NIC, PAN, SLV (central-america) and BMU, CAN, GRL, SPM, USA (northern-america). Every unit was checked for decolonisation, renames, transfers, occupations and claims since 1945, and NE footprints were compared with the history (island ownership, the NIC/CRI line, Hans Island, Swan Islands, Sapodilla Cayes, uncovered minor islands). Tier-1 overlays: Guatemalan claim to Belize, Sapodilla Cayes, Swan Islands, Gulf of Fonseca islands (El Tigre, Meanguera; 1980–1991), Hans Island; indigenous comarcas of Panama. Note entries record editorial decisions, sub-granularity events and known gaps (Nicaraguan administration north of the Coco to 1961, El Salvador–Honduras bolsones, islands NE leaves outside admin-0, US reservations, Canadian, Central American and Mexican indigenous lands). Audited 2026-10-01 by agent:audit-modern-north-central-america; analysis scripts in .cache/audit/audit-modern-north-central-america.

| id | op | polity / target | years | reason | sources | confidence | status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `m-north-central-america-0001` | note |  | 1946–present | Editorial decisions for this file. (a) pids: ne:<code> for every unit that exists today, including dependencies (ne:bmu, ne:grl, ne:spm, ne:clp, with kind 'dependency' and power = the administering state); ovr: pids for former dependencies (ovr:british-honduras – one pid across the 1973 rename to Belize –, ovr:dominion-of-newfoundland, ovr:territory-of-alaska, ovr:territory-of-hawaii, ovr:panama-canal-zone). (b) The territories of Alaska and Hawaii (1946–1958) and the Panama Canal Zone are 'dependency' because the United States reported them to the UN as non-self-governing territories (GA res. 66 (I), 1946). (c) Greenland stays 'dependency' of Denmark for 1953–1978 although the 1953 constitution made it an integral county (amt) of Denmark: kept as one Danish-coloured unit so the 1953, 1979 and 2009 status changes show as periods; the period notes say what each status was. Likewise Saint Pierre and Miquelon stays a French 'dependency' (ne:spm) in its years as an overseas department (1976–1984), with periods at its 1976, 1985 and 2003 status changes. (d) Great Swan Island (1946–1971) is given to the United States itself (ne:usa) in tier 0 with a tier-1 'disputed' overlay, because it had no separate government. (e) Tier-1 'disputed' overlays: controller = the tier-0 holder, claimants = the states contesting it. | [1](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_66_(1%29) [2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_list_of_non-self-governing_territories) [3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenland) [4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon) [5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swan_Islands,_Honduras) | high | active |
| `m-north-central-america-0005` | note |  | 1946–1966 | Chamizal (El Paso – Ciudad Juárez): the tract the 1911 arbitration had largely given to Mexico was transferred by the Chamizal Convention (29 August 1963) and handed over on 28 October 1967 (366 acres of the Chamizal and 264 acres east of Cordova Island to Mexico; 193 acres of Cordova Island to the United States), with the Rio Grande moved into a new channel. Natural Earth draws the present line, so 1946–1966 show the ~2.5 km2 as Mexican. Deliberately not corrected: far below the dataset's 1,000 km2 granularity and below Natural Earth's ~1 km precision. The later minor exchanges under the 1970 Boundary Treaty are also not drawn. | [6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chamizal_dispute) | high | active |
| `m-north-central-america-0006` | note |  | 2010–2018 | Isla Portillos (Costa Rica–Nicaragua): Nicaraguan troops occupied about 3 km2 at the northern tip of Isla Portillos in late 2010. The ICJ ordered provisional measures in March 2011, found on 16 December 2015 that the disputed territory is Costa Rican, and on 2 February 2018 that Costa Rica holds the whole northern part of Isla Portillos except Harbor Head Lagoon and its sandbar (Nicaraguan), ordering a Nicaraguan military camp removed. Natural Earth's NIC/CRI line already matches the 2018 judgment, with the lagoon as a separate 1.2 km2 NIC part. The 2010–2013 occupation is below the dataset's granularity; not drawn. | [7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isla_Calero) [8](https://www.icj-cij.org/case/165) [9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Costa_Rica) | high | active |
| `m-north-central-america-0007` | note |  | 1946–1970 | Corn Islands (Nicaragua): leased to the United States for 99 years by the Bryan–Chamorro Treaty (signed 5 August 1914, ratified 1916), which also gave an option for a naval base in the Gulf of Fonseca. The treaty was abolished by a 1970 convention. No source shows US occupation or administration, so the islands stay Nicaraguan throughout. | [10](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bryan%E2%80%93Chamorro_Treaty) | high | active |
| `m-north-central-america-0008` | note |  | 1946–present | Machias Seal Island and North Rock (Gulf of Maine): Canadian lighthouse and permanent presence since 1832, claimed by the United States; left in the 'grey zone' by the 1984 ICJ Gulf of Maine judgment. Not drawn because the 8 ha island is not in Natural Earth's land or minor-islands layers (nearest NE land 17.8 km away). If a future land layer includes it, give it to Canada with a 'disputed' overlay (claimant ne:usa). | [11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Machias_Seal_Island) | high | active |
| `m-north-central-america-0009` | note |  | 2022–present | PROPOSED – Hans Island (Tartupaluk) land boundary of the 14 June 2022 agreement: the line follows the ravine running north–south across the island; the western ~40% goes to Canada (Nunavut), the eastern ~60% to Denmark/Greenland. Not applied because the sources consulted confirm Denmark's ratification (19 December 2023) but not Canada's (still pending in February 2025; the Arctic Foreign Policy of 6 December 2024 promised to finalize it) nor the entry into force, and because Natural Earth's islet (1.44 km2, NE disputed area B21) lies about 2.1 km east of the true island. Wikidata gives (unreferenced) land-boundary points (-66.4507, 80.8214) T1, (-66.4645, 80.8259) T2, (-66.4567, 80.8318) T3 (lon, lat). Shifted by +0.1191 deg longitude onto NE's islet they would cut it about 51/49 rather than 40/60, so NE's island shape is too rough for an exact split. When entry into force is confirmed, add a GRL subunit 'Hans Island west' held by ne:can from that year, using a polygon west of the shifted line (verify against the treaty annex coordinates and the official datum), or wait for a land layer that draws the island correctly. | [12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans_Island) [13](https://www.canada.ca/en/global-affairs/news/2022/06/boundary-dispute.html) [14](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whisky_War) [15](https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Q139677272) [16](https://legionmagazine.com/canada-announces-arctic-foreign-policy-overhaul/) | medium | proposed |
| `m-north-central-america-0011` | note |  | 1946–present | Panama Canal Zone polygon (PAN subunit, 1946–1978): approximate construction from the treaty definition, checked against two georeferenced US government maps. (1) Canal centreline = Natural Earth 'Panama Canal' centrelines (ne_10m_rivers_lake_centerlines), extended along the dredged approach channels to the Limón Bay breakwater (79.920 W) and the Pacific channel past Flamenco Island (to 8.898 N 79.540 W), then 3 marine miles out to sea. (2) A band 5 statute miles (8,046.72 m) each side with square ends. (3) Plus Natural Earth's Gatun Lake widened by 300 m for the 100-ft contour ('Canal Zone boundary line follows 100 ft. contour around Gatun Lake' – 1920 map). (4) Plus Madden Lake (Alhajuela, 1935), digitized from the 1947 Army Map Service sheet (59 km2) and widened by 150 m for the ~260-ft contour named on that sheet, and the Chagres River corridor from the band to Madden Dam (300 m buffer). (5) Minus the city of Colón (north part of Manzanillo Island) and the city of Panama east of the Curundú line (79°32.2'W 9°02.2'N to 79°33.8'W 8°57.2'N, read off the 1920 map). Result: 1,370 km2 on NE land (official total 1,432 km2 including water). Compared with the 1920 map the band edges lie within about 1 km of the drawn boundary (west edge on 80°00'W, east edge near 79°51'W at the Atlantic; Arraiján just outside). Not drawn: the Colón corridor (1950) and the small 1955 Remón–Eisenhower land returns. After 1 October 1979 the zone ceased to exist and Panama took jurisdiction; until 31 December 1999 the United States kept canal operating areas and defence sites under the treaties. These were scattered parcels without an open map, so they are not drawn and the land shows as Panama from 1979. Sources are public-domain US government maps (1920 Panama Canal map signed by Governor Chester Harding; 1947 US Army Map Service), georeferenced on their printed graticules; scripts in .cache/audit/audit-modern-north-central-america (a05–a08). | [17](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panama_Canal_Zone) [18](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zona_del_Canal_de_Panam%C3%A1) [19](https://www.lindahall.org/experience/digital-exhibitions/the-land-divided-the-world-united/15-glossary) [20](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Map_of_the_Panama_Canal_Zone_-_NARA_-_6861830.jpg) [21](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Panama_-_Canal_Zone_and_vicinity.jpg) [22](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Panama_Canal_Zone.jpg) [23](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madden_Dam) [24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torrijos%E2%80%93Carter_Treaties) | medium | active |

| `m-north-central-america-0016` | note |  | 1999–present | Nunavut and Inuit self-government in Canada: Nunavut was split from the Northwest Territories on 1 April 1999 under the Nunavut Act and the Nunavut Land Claims Agreement Act (both 1993). It has a public territorial government for all residents, not an Inuit government, so it is neither a separate polity nor an indigenous overlay here: internal provincial and territorial boundaries are not drawn in the modern layer. The Inuit regional governments and settlement lands (Nunatsiavut, Inuvialuit, Nunavik, Inuit Owned Lands) are covered by the known gap on Canadian indigenous lands. | [26](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nunavut) [27](https://www.rcaanc-cirnac.gc.ca/eng/1677073191939/1677073214344) | high | active |
| `m-north-central-america-0022` | note |  | 1992–present | Conejo Island (Isla Conejo, Gulf of Fonseca, 13°20′52″N 87°44′34″W): a small islet administered by Honduras (department of Valle) with a military post and claimed by El Salvador (La Unión). El Salvador had asked the ICJ Chamber to settle every island of the Gulf, but the 1992 judgment ruled only on El Tigre, Meanguera and Meanguerita, and the dispute over Conejo continues. Not drawn because the islet is not in Natural Earth's land (nearest NE land, the Honduran coast, is 1.7 km away). If a future land layer includes it, leave it to Honduras with a 'disputed' overlay (claimant ne:slv). | [28](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conejo_Island) [29](https://www.icj-cij.org/case/75) | high | active |

The island (a few km²) is not separate Natural Earth land — the NE VEN/GUY line runs through solid land here — so the Venezuelan-held Guyanese half cannot be drawn without a hand-traced island outline. A future editor with a sourced outline should add a GUY subunit (1966–present: ne:ven) plus a disputed overlay (controller ne:ven, claimant ne:guy). | [Ankoko Island (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ankoko_Island); [Guayana Esequiba (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guayana_Esequiba) |

Natural Earth draws the post-award line; pre-1966 claim lines are not available as open geometry, so the dispute is not drawn. | [DIFROL (Chile) — Controversia entre los hitos 16 y 17: Arbitraje Palena](https://controversiasinternacionales.difrol.cl/casos/arbitraje-palena/) |

| `m-caribbean-south-america-0127` | modern/caribbean-south-america.json |  | 1946–2026 | ISLAND FIX NEEDED (pipeline) — the largest uncovered Natural Earth land part in this region, a 33 km² minor-island polygon at -58.31,-34.20 (bounds 58.345–58.282 W, 34.262–34.136 S; 4.3 km from ARG, 9.5 km from URY), is the advancing front of the Paraná delta in the Río de la Plata: Isla Oyarvide (34°12′30″S 58°20′30″W), Islas Solís and Islas Lucía (34°10′S 58°20′W), all Argentine (partido de San Fernando, Buenos Aires; under Argentine sovereignty after the Río de la Plata Treaty of 1973). Both ARG and URY lie within the 10-km near radius and 9.5 < 3 × 4.3, so coast rule (b) leaves it ambiguous and it stays unclaimed in every year; assign it to the ARG timeline (ne:arg) for 1946–present. These are recent alluvial islands (Islas Lucía were named in 2001), so the static land base overstates them in the early years. The 1906 award of the King of Spain made the Coco (Segovia, Wanks) the boundary, but Nicaragua rejected it and kept administering land north of the river. Honduras created the department of Gracias a Dios there in February 1957, which led to the Mocorón clashes (May 1957). The ICJ upheld the award on 18 November 1960. Under the OAS Basis of Arrangement of March 1961, Nicaraguan authorities evacuated about 8,700 km2 (IBS 36; 'more than eight thousand square kilometres' in Nicaragua's 2001 memorial), which Honduras took over by May 1961; a further 17.5 km2 at Teotecacinte was settled on 5 August 1961. Natural Earth's HND unit therefore shows Honduras in 1946–1960 on land Nicaragua held. Not drawn because no source found describes the line of Nicaraguan administration. Honduras (ICJ case 120), Memorial of Nicaragua (2001), ch. Panama, beyond the three NE-admin-1 comarcas drawn here: Madungandí (Law 24 of 12 January 1996, ~2,076 km2, above granularity), Wargandí (Law 34 of 25 July 2000, 956 km2), Naso Tjër Di (Law 188 of 4 December 2020, 1,606 km2). Mexico: the Zapatista autonomous municipalities and caracoles in Chiapas (since the 1994 uprising, reorganised in 2023) are de facto self-governing communities without fixed boundaries; ejidos and agrarian communities are tenure, not polities. | [Indigenous peoples in Costa Rica (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_in_Costa_Rica); [Madungandí (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madungand%C3%AD); [Guna de Wargandí (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guna_de_Wargand%C3%AD); [Naso Tjër Di Comarca (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naso_Tj%C3%ABr_Di_Comarca); [CARICOM, "CCJ grants historic constitutional relief to Maya people" – Maya Leaders Alliance v. Attorney General of Belize \[2015\] CCJ 15 (AJ), 30 October 2015](https://caricom.org/ccj-grants-historic-constitutional-relief-to-maya-people/); [Rebel Zapatista Autonomous Municipalities (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebel_Zapatista_Autonomous_Municipalities) |
